Writing the discovery tests: unit tests for the selection rules and the price-file rule, plus one that replays the recorded CDX answers and checks the result equals the committed manifest:

**Tool call:** `Write` <sub>toolu_01UtJFTt7eBHMrCSmgesCxqU</sub>

- `file_path`: `~/parcel-rates-issue-13/data/tests/test_discover.py`
- `content`:

```
"""Source discovery (data/collect/discover.py, sources.py, wayback.py), offline.

The rules are tested on small hand-made CDX answers. The last test replays the recorded
CDX answers in fixtures/cdx.json.gz through the whole catalogue and checks that discovery
rebuilds the committed manifest row for row: refresh the recording with
`uv run python -m data.collect.discover --record` when the catalogue changes.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import pytest

from data.collect import discover as d
from data.collect import wayback as w
from data.collect.sources import SOURCES, usps_price_file


def cap(ts: str, status: str = "200", mimetype: str = "application/pdf", digest: str = "A", original: str = "x") -> w.Capture:
    return w.Capture("key", ts, original, status, mimetype, digest)


CAPTURES = [
    cap("20240101120000", digest="OLD"),
    cap("20240301120000", status="403", mimetype="text/html", digest="BLOCK"),
    cap("20240401120000", digest="NEW"),
    cap("20240405120000", status="-", mimetype="warc/revisit", digest="NEW"),
    cap("20240410120000", status="-", mimetype="warc/revisit", digest="BLOCK"),
    cap("20240501120000", mimetype="text/html", digest="PAGE"),
]


def test_stamp_pads_dates_to_cdx_timestamps():
    assert w.stamp("2024-04-02") == "20240402000000"
    assert w.stamp("20240402123456") == "20240402123456"
    assert w.stamp("") == ""


def test_select_takes_the_latest_good_capture_before_the_cut_off():
    assert w.select(CAPTURES, w.Pick(before="2024-04-02")).timestamp == "20240401120000"
    assert w.select(CAPTURES, w.Pick(before="2024-03-31")).timestamp == "20240101120000"  # the 403 is skipped


def test_select_keeps_revisits_of_good_bytes_only():
    # The revisit of NEW counts; the revisit of the 403 block page does not.
    assert w.select(CAPTURES, w.Pick(before="2024-04-30")).timestamp == "20240405120000"


def test_select_honours_after_earliest_and_mimetype():
    assert w.select(CAPTURES, w.Pick(after="2024-02-01", latest=False)).timestamp == "20240401120000"
    assert w.select(CAPTURES, w.Pick(mimetype="pdf")).timestamp == "20240401120000"
    assert w.select(CAPTURES, w.Pick(before="2023-12-31")) is None


def test_id_url_and_query_window():
    assert w.id_url("20240401120000", "https://a.example/f.pdf") == "https://web.archive.org/web/20240401120000id_/https://a.example/f.pdf"
    q = w.query_string("a.example/f.pdf", pick=w.Pick(before="2024-04-02"))
    assert "to=20240402000000" in q and "from=" not in q


FOLDER = [
    cap("20240409204843", original="https://pe.usps.com/resources/PriceChange/July%202024%20Price%20Change%20040924%20-%20Notice%20123.zip"),
    cap("20240511035537", original="https://pe.usps.com/resources/PriceChange/July%202024%20Price%20Change%20Draft%20Redlines%20-%20051024.zip"),
    cap("20240710113432", original="https://pe.usps.com/resources/PriceChange/July%202024%20Price%20Change%2020240614%20-%20Notice%20123.zip"),
    cap("20240720000000", original="https://pe.usps.com/resources/PriceChange/July%202024%20Price%20Change%20-%20SKU%20Price%20Files.zip"),
    cap("20240710113412", original="https://pe.usps.com/resources/PriceChange/July%202024%20Price%20Change%2020240625%20-%20Notice123.pdf"),
    cap("20220415154804", original="https://pe.usps.com/resources/PriceChange/July%202022%20-%20Price%20Files%20-%20CSV.zip"),
    cap("20250412192722", original="https://pe.usps.com/resources/PriceChange/July%202025%20Price%20Change%2020250411%20-%20Notice%20123%20-%20HPM%20and%20no%20BPM.zip"),
]


def test_usps_price_file_takes_the_last_final_notice_123_zip():
    assert usps_price_file("July 2024", FOLDER) == (
        "https://pe.usps.com/resources/PriceChange/July%202024%20Price%20Change%2020240614%20-%20Notice%20123.zip"
    )
    assert usps_price_file("July 2022", FOLDER).endswith("/July%202022%20-%20Price%20Files%20-%20CSV.zip")
    assert usps_price_file("July 2025", FOLDER) is None  # only an alternative scenario
    assert usps_price_file("July 202", FOLDER) is None  # labels match whole words


def test_catalogue_paths_are_unique_and_archived_entries_have_a_rule():
    paths = [s.path for s in SOURCES]
    assert len(paths) == len(set(paths))
    assert all(s.pick is not None and s.pick.before for s in SOURCES if s.original)
    assert all(s.url or s.original or s.price_label for s in SOURCES)


def test_differences_report_changes_additions_and_removals():
    old = [{"path": "a", "url": "1", "method": "direct", "in_git": "true", "content": "x", "carrier": "c", "year": ""},
           {"path": "b", "url": "2", "method": "direct", "in_git": "true", "content": "x", "carrier": "c", "year": ""}]
    new = [dict(old[0], url="9"), {"path": "c", "url": "3"}]
    assert d.differences(new, old) == ["~ a  url: '1' -> '9'", "+ c  3", "- b"]


@pytest.mark.skipif(not d.FIXTURE.is_file(), reason="no recorded CDX answers")
def test_discovery_rebuilds_the_committed_manifest():
    old = d.read_manifest()
    rows, problems = d.discover(w.RecordedCdx(d.FIXTURE), SOURCES, {r["path"]: r for r in old}, log=lambda *_: None)
    assert problems == []
    assert d.differences(rows, old) == []
    assert rows == old  # checksums carried over, same order

```

```
def amounts_in(quote: str) -> list[str]:
    """Dollar amounts in a quote, in order, as two-decimal strings."""
    return [m.replace(",", "") for m in re.findall(r"\$\s?([\d,]+\.\d{2})", quote)]
```

```
packages for each service level is the highest applicable surcharge for the service level. For example, if the shipper has volume in a week of 175% of the Baseline average weekly volume for UPS Ground Residential, then
each UPS Ground Residential package will be assessed a surcharge of $2.70 (not just those over 150% of Baseline average weekly volume for UPS Ground Residential). For holiday weeks that are not full UPS operating weeks,
the surcharge will apply to all packages in that weekly invoice period for the applicable service level multiplied by the number of full UPS operating days in that week. Customer’s volume includes volume from any

6

weekly volume from August 31, 2025 through September 27, 2025 will be used as the Baseline volume.
```

```
    ]

def test_peak_rows_outside_their_window_do_not_apply(tables):
    # The 2025-labelled UPS peak rows cover the 2024-25 season only.
    parcel = Parcel(weight_lb=2, distance_miles=700, residential=True, speed="ground")
    q = quote_service(tables, tables.services["ups_ground"], parcel, Controls(year=2025, peak=True))
    assert not any(li.condition and li.condition.startswith("peak_") for li in q.line_items)
    q24 = quote_service(tables, tables.services["ups_ground"], parcel, Controls(year=2024, peak=True))
    assert any(li.condition == "peak_residential" for li in q24.line_items)


def test_usps_2026_transportation_increase_by_window(tables):
    parcel = Parcel(weight_lb=2, distance_miles=700, residential=True, speed="ground")
    sid = "usps_ground_advantage"
    jan = quote_service(tables, tables.services[sid], parcel, Controls(year=2026))
    assert [li.kind for li in jan.line_items] == ["base"]
    may = quote_service(tables, tables.services[sid], parcel, Controls(year=2026, as_of=date(2026, 5, 1)))
    row = [r for r in read_csv("usps/surcharges.csv") if r["condition"] == "transportation_time_limited"][0]
    base = may.line_items[0].amount
    assert may.line_items[1].surcharge_id == row["surcharge_id"]
    assert may.line_items[1].amount == cents(base * Decimal(row["amount"]) / 100)


# ---- dimensional weight over time -------------------------------------------
def _usps_divisor(as_of: str) -> int:
```

```
  FedEx do not let a script collect their zone charts, so their zones are
  estimated from the distance between the two ZIPs (or the miles typed); the
  measured accuracy is in [docs/data/ZONE_ACCURACY.md](docs/data/ZONE_ACCURACY.md).
- The UPS fuel surcharge default is a proxy taken from FedEx's published
  history (no UPS history is retrievable); the slider overrides it.
- UPS announced increases come from trade press, not from UPS itself.
- UPS peak (demand) surcharge documents for the 2025-26 and 2026-27 seasons
  are not in the sources, so UPS 2025 and 2026 peak quotes carry no peak
  charges.
- FedEx 2027 surcharges are incomplete (only the Home Delivery residential
  charge and January peak rows), so 2027 totals understate surcharges.
- Peak surcharges are a toggle that prices on December 10 of the year, not by
  ship date.
- UPS and FedEx bill a large or oversize package at a minimum of 90 lb. The
  minimum is modelled (issue #18), but actual weight is still capped at 70 lb;
  parcels whose dimensional weight is 71–150 lb are priced from the carriers'
  tables.
- Weight is capped at 70 lb, the USPS maximum. Alaska and Hawaii are out of
  scope.
- UPS 2024 and 2026 use the mid-year revision of the Daily Rates guide,
  because the December original could not be retrieved.

The same list is returned by `GET /api/meta` and shown in the app.

## What I would improve next

Open issues in the repository, in rough priority order:

- #14 capture the UPS 2025-26 and 2026-27 demand surcharge documents.
- Real UPS and FedEx zones through their official rating APIs (with the
  shop's own API keys), replacing the distance estimate where keys are given.
- #13 script the source discovery so data collection runs end to end without
  an agent.
```

```
330:## How this was built: the agent log
mostly the bulky boxes, parcels over 5 lb and the far zones. Every row also
carries an illustrative `order_value` (about $9 per lb plus a basket of other
items); with free shipping from $75, 54 parcels ship free and the USPS margin
falls to −$2.06 per parcel.

## Data provenance

All prices come from the carriers' own published rate documents:

| Carrier | Base rates | Surcharge rules | Years |
|---|---|---|---|
| USPS | Postal Explorer CSV zips per price change; Notice 123 PDFs | Notice 123 footnotes; Domestic Mail Manual for dimensional weight | 2023 (July tables, Ground Advantage launch) to 2026 |
| UPS | Daily Rates guide PDF per year | Surcharge section of the same guide; demand surcharge PDFs | 2023 to 2026 |
| FedEx | Standard List Rates XLSX (PDF for 2023 and 2024) | Yearly surcharge-change PDFs and Service Guides | 2023 to 2026, plus 2027 as a forward year |

**Collection.** Every raw file is listed in
[data/raw/manifest.csv](data/raw/manifest.csv) with its URL, retrieval method,
SHA-256 and size, and each carrier folder has a `SOURCES.md` describing what
each file is. `python3 data/fetch_raw.py` restores missing files one at a
time, with a pause and jitter between requests, backoff on errors, an
identifying User-Agent and checksum verification. UPS and FedEx block direct
Shell cwd was reset to ~/parcel-rates
```

````
identifying User-Agent and checksum verification. UPS and FedEx block direct
downloads from scripts, so 78 of the 135 files are fetched through the Wayback
Machine (`method=wayback`).

**What is committed.** Spreadsheets, CSV zips and small HTML snapshots (58
files, about 9 MB) are in git. The PDFs (77 files, about 137 MB) are not; run
`python3 data/fetch_raw.py` to get them. The processed tables in
`data/processed/` are committed, so the app and the backend tests never need
the raw files.

**Regenerating `data/processed/`** (needs the PDFs, so fetch first):

```sh
python3 data/fetch_raw.py                     # standard library only
uv run python -m data.pipeline.usps
uv run python -m data.pipeline.ups
uv run python -m data.pipeline.fedex
uv run python -m data.pipeline.report_changes # rewrites docs/data/RULE_CHANGES.md
```

**ZIP codes and zones** (issue #9): the USPS zone chart for every origin ZIP3
comes from the USPS Domestic Zone Chart tool (about an hour, polite and
resumable; the raw charts stay out of git and are checked against
`data/raw/usps/zones/charts.csv`), the ZIP points from the Census ZCTA
Gazetteer, and the accuracy report compares the distance estimate with the
real USPS charts and the UPS charts archived for 2026:

```sh
uv run python -m data.collect.usps_zones      # data/processed/usps/zone_chart.csv
uv run python -m data.collect.zip_points      # data/processed/zip_points.csv
uv run python -m data.collect.zone_accuracy   # docs/data/ZONE_ACCURACY.md
```

**Next-year announcements** arrive before the price lists. A watcher finds
them by rule (the USPS January price release, the dated UPS GRI table, the
FedEx rate-changes page), live first and then the latest Wayback capture,
saves each source to `data/raw` with a manifest row, and records the
percentage with its verbatim quote in `data/collect/announcements_found.csv`,
which the carrier pipelines merge:

```sh
uv run python -m data.collect.announcements --check   # report what is announced for next year
uv run python -m data.collect.announcements           # record it, then rerun data.pipeline.<carrier>
```

Base rates are parsed. Surcharges, fuel defaults, announced increases,
dimensional weight rules and the distance-to-zone bands are curated by hand
in `data/pipeline/*_rules.py`, each row with the source file, page and a
verbatim quote. Details: [docs/data/QUALITY.md](docs/data/QUALITY.md) (survey
and decisions), [docs/data/SCHEMA.md](docs/data/SCHEMA.md) (tables) and
[docs/data/RULE_CHANGES.md](docs/data/RULE_CHANGES.md) (every rule that
changed between years).
Shell cwd was reset to ~/parcel-rates
````

So far, for #13 and #14, all on branch `issue-13-collect`:

- **Wayback CDX client** (`data/collect/wayback.py`): capture-selection rules, polite fetching, and record/replay so tests run offline.
- **Source catalogue** (`data/collect/sources.py`): every manifest file plus the rule that finds it. USPS price-file names are found from the Wayback listing by rule, and that rule reproduces all 15 existing picks.
- **Discovery script** (`data/collect/discover.py`): rebuilds the manifest, with `--check`, `--write`, `--newer` and recording.
- **#14**: Wayback does have UPS's demand-surcharge PDFs for 2025-26 (August 28, 2025 update) and 2026-27 (August 26, 2026 update). UPS moved them to a new address in 2025. I've transcribed both into the UPS rules and removed the "not in the sources" notes.

```
import gzip, json, hashlib, base64, csv, re, sys
sys.path.insert(0,'.')
from data.collect import wayback as w
from data.collect.sources import SOURCES
rec=json.load(gzip.open('$S/cdx-live.json.gz','rt'))
man={r['path']:r for r in csv.DictReader(open('data/raw/manifest.csv'))}
paths=['fedex/2022/rate-changes_page_wayback_2022-03-24.html','fedex/2022/surcharges-and-fees_page_wayback_2022-01-05.html','fedex/2024/rate-changes_page_wayback_2024-04-07.html','fedex/2025/Service_Guide_2025.pdf','fedex/2023/peak-surcharges_page_wayback_2022-11.html','fedex/2024/demand-surcharges_page_wayback_2023-12.html','fedex/2025/demand-surcharges_page_wayback_2024-11.html','fedex/2026/demand-surcharges_page_wayback_2025-11.html','fedex/2027/demand-surcharges_page_wayback_2026-10.html']
for p in paths:
    src=next(s for s in SOURCES if s.path==p)
    data=open('data/raw/'+p,'rb').read()
    dig=base64.b32encode(hashlib.sha1(data).digest()).decode()
    caps=w.parse(rec[w.query_string(src.original, pick=src.pick)])
    mts=re.search(r'/web/(\d{14})id_',man[p]['url']).group(1)
    hit=[c.timestamp for c in caps if c.digest==dig]
    inman=[c for c in caps if c.timestamp==mts]
    print(p, 'manifest ts', mts, 'in cdx:', [(c.status,c.digest==dig) for c in inman], 'digest hits:', hit[-3:], 'n', len(caps))
EOF
```

```
    b=urllib.request.urlopen(urllib.request.Request(u,headers={'User-Agent':UA}),timeout=180).read()
    if b[:2]==b'\x1f\x8b': b=gzip.decompress(b)
    print(hashlib.sha256(b).hexdigest()==man[p]['sha256'], p)
    time.sleep(4)
EOF
```

```
  from committed scripts without an LLM or agent, so the data can be rebuilt
```

````
identifying User-Agent and checksum verification. UPS and FedEx block direct
downloads from scripts, so 78 of the 135 files are fetched through the Wayback

**What is committed.** Spreadsheets, CSV zips and small HTML snapshots (58

`python3 data/fetch_raw.py` to get them.''','''**Collection.** Every raw file is listed in
[data/raw/manifest.csv](data/raw/manifest.csv) with its URL, retrieval method,
SHA-256 and size, and each carrier folder has a `SOURCES.md` describing what
each file is. No step needs an agent: two commands rebuild `data/raw/` and the
manifest from a clean clone.

```sh
uv run python -m data.collect.discover --write   # find every source, write the manifest
python3 data/fetch_raw.py                        # download what is not in git, check every checksum
```

Discovery (issue #13) works from the catalogue in
[data/collect/sources.py](data/collect/sources.py): each file's publisher URL
and the rule that picks it. UPS and FedEx block scripted downloads and replace
files in place, so 85 of the 143 files come from the Wayback Machine
(`method=wayback`). For those, discovery asks the archive's CDX index and takes
the latest good capture before the entry's cut-off. USPS price-file names are
read from the CDX listing of Postal Explorer's price-change folder (the last
final "Notice 123" zip of each price change). Both scripts are sequential and
polite: a 3 to 5 s pause with jitter, backoff on 429 and 5xx, and an
identifying User-Agent. The fetcher never re-downloads a file whose checksum
matches. `--check` (the default) compares discovery with the committed manifest
without writing anything. `--newer` also lists captures newer than each
cut-off, which is where a refresh starts. The tests replay recorded CDX answers
(`data/tests/fixtures/cdx.json.gz`), so they check offline that discovery
rebuilds the committed manifest row for row.

**What is committed.** Spreadsheets, CSV zips and small HTML snapshots (64
files, about 11 MB) are in git. The PDFs (79 files, about 137 MB) are not; run
`python3 data/fetch_raw.py` to get them.''')
rep('''- UPS peak (demand) surcharge documents for the 2025-26 and 2026-27 seasons
  are not in the sources, so UPS 2025 and 2026 peak quotes carry no peak
  charges.
''','')
rep('''- #14 capture the UPS 2025-26 and 2026-27 demand surcharge documents.
- Real UPS and FedEx zones through their official rating APIs (with the
  shop's own API keys), replacing the distance estimate where keys are given.
- #13 script the source discovery so data collection runs end to end without
  an agent.''','''- Real UPS and FedEx zones through their official rating APIs (with the
  shop's own API keys), replacing the distance estimate where keys are given.
- A scheduled discovery run with `--newer`, so a new carrier document opens a
  pull request instead of waiting for someone to look.''')
open(p,'w').write(s)
EOF
````

```
CATALOGUE = Path(__file__).resolve().parent / "sources.csv"


def load_catalogue(path: Path = CATALOGUE) -> list[Source]:
    """Every other source, one row each: a direct URL, or a publisher URL and its cut-off."""
    with path.open(newline="", encoding="utf-8") as handle:
```

```
"""The raw-source catalogue: every file in data/raw/manifest.csv, and the rule that finds it.

Discovery (data/collect/discover.py) turns these entries into manifest rows with no agent
involved. The USPS price changes are a pattern in code below; every other source is a row
of data/collect/sources.csv (method, the URL, the cut-off for archived files, whether a
direct page is live, whether the file is kept in git, and its description). Three kinds
of entry:

- `direct`: a URL on the publisher's own site that serves the file as is. Entries marked
  `live` point at a page that is replaced in place (a "current" price list, a DMM section),
  so a later fetch can return newer bytes; their copies are kept in git.
- `archived`: a file taken from the Wayback Machine. The entry gives the publisher's URL
  and a `Pick` (the latest good capture before a cut-off date); discovery asks the CDX
  index and emits the id_ passthrough URL of the capture the rule selects. The cut-offs
  reproduce the captures the data were built from (a full timestamp, one second after the
  capture, where the archive holds several captures that day); to take a newer version,
  move the cut-off and re-run discovery, then the fetch and the data tests.
- USPS price changes: one entry per price change in `USPS_PRICE_CHANGES` gives its
  Notice 123 PDF (the DMM archive folder named after the effective date) and its price-file
  zip, whose file name is discovered from the CDX listing of Postal Explorer's PriceChange
  folder (see `usps_price_file`).

Order matters: it is the order of the manifest rows.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import csv
import re
import urllib.parse
from dataclasses import dataclass
from pathlib import Path

from data.collect.wayback import Capture, Pick

USPS_DMM_ARCHIVE = "https://pe.usps.com/Archive/PDF/DMMArchive{date}/Notice123.pdf"
USPS_PRICE_FOLDER = "pe.usps.com/resources/PriceChange/"


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Source:
    carrier: str
    year: str
    path: str
    content: str
    in_git: bool
    url: str = ""  # direct: the file itself
    original: str = ""  # archived: the publisher's URL
    pick: Pick | None = None
    live: bool = False  # direct, and replaced in place by the publisher

143 rows, 0 differences from the manifest, 0 unresolved
All checks passed!
9 passed in 0.09s
```

````
## #13: source discovery without an agent

Two commands now rebuild `data/raw/` and the manifest from a clean clone:

```sh
uv run python -m data.collect.discover --write   # find every source, write the manifest
python3 data/fetch_raw.py                        # download what is not in git, check every checksum
```

- **`data/collect/sources.csv`**: the catalogue, one row per source: publisher URL, method, Wayback cut-off, live flag, in-git flag and description.
- **`data/collect/sources.py`**: the rules, and the USPS price changes as a pattern. Each price change gives its Notice 123 PDF (DMM archive folder named after the effective date) and its price-file zip. The zip's name is **discovered** from the CDX listing of Postal Explorer's PriceChange folder: the last final "Notice 123" or "Price Files - CSV" zip, leaving out SKU files, drafts, redlines, proposals and the alternative scenarios. That rule reproduces all 15 existing picks.
- **`data/collect/wayback.py`**: the CDX client (sequential, 3 to 5 s pause with jitter, backoff on 429/5xx, identifying User-Agent) and capture selection: the latest good capture before a cut-off. Revisits count only when they repeat good bytes.
- **`data/collect/discover.py`**: `--check` (default) compares with the committed manifest, `--write` rewrites it, `--newer` lists captures after each cut-off (where a refresh starts), `--record` and `--reuse` work with CDX recordings, and `--offline` replays one.
- **Offline test:** `data/tests/test_discover.py` replays `data/tests/fixtures/cdx.json.gz` (143 KB) and checks that discovery rebuilds the committed manifest row for row, along with unit tests of the rules.

As you chose, the cut-offs **reproduce the captures the data were built from**. A live run reproduced 131 of 141 rows straight away. These seven rows now have a new URL but the **same bytes**, each checked against its SHA-256:
- 5 FedEx pages and 1 UPS PDF had nearest-capture timestamps (`…000000`, or one with no capture behind it) instead of real captures. One of them, the 2024-25 UPS demand PDF, now answers 403. They point at the real captures now.
- The October 2026 Notice 123 came from USPS's "current" `notice123.pdf`, which is replaced at every price change. It now uses the identical file's stable Postal Explorer name.

The two remaining differences are the new #14 rows.

## #14: UPS demand surcharges for 2025-26 and 2026-27

UPS moved its demand-surcharge PDF to `assets.ups.com` in 2025, and the old URL redirects there. Wayback has the **August 28, 2025** and **August 26, 2026** updates. Both are in the catalogue and manifest, and their windows are transcribed into `ups_rules.py` with page and quote. `PEAK[year]` is now a list of documents: a window belongs to every price year in force during part of it. On December 10, UPS Ground Residential now carries $0.60 (2025) and $0.75 (2026). Before, there was no charge.

The two "not in the sources" exception notes and the limitation (README, `/api/meta`, API examples, fixtures) are removed. `RULE_CHANGES.md` is regenerated.
````

```
from PIL import Image
src='frontend/e2e/screenshots/'; dst='docs/screenshots/'
def footer_top(im):
    g=im.convert('L'); w,h=g.size; px=g.load()
    for y in range(h-1, h//3, -1):
        dark=sum(1 for x in range(0,w,4) if px[x,y]<60)
        if dark > 0.6*(w/4): return y
    return h
def save(im,name):
    im.convert('RGB').quantize(256, method=Image.Quantize.MEDIANCUT, dither=Image.Dither.NONE).save(dst+name, optimize=True)
for s,d in [('q1-typical','q1-quote'),('q2','q2-breakeven'),('q3','q3-blended'),('q4','q4-yearly')]:
    im=Image.open(src+f'{s}-desktop-light.png'); y=footer_top(im)
    im=im.crop((0,0,im.width,y-16)); im=im.resize((1200, round(im.height*1200/im.width)), Image.LANCZOS)
    save(im,f'{d}-desktop.png')
for s,d in [('q1-typical-mobile-light','q1-quote-mobile'),('q3-mobile-dark','q3-blended-mobile-dark')]:
    im=Image.open(src+s+'.png'); save(im.crop((0,0,750,1624)), d+'.png')
EOF
```
